## Part 1. Measurement scripts (run on production, in the in-app browser)

Open `https://lsatprep.study/classroom/drill` signed in as Joshua (he is signed in on that browser). Run each script with the browser's JavaScript tool. Record the output verbatim in the report.

### 1a. Contrast scan

```js
(() => {
  const L = ([r,g,b]) => { const f = c => { c /= 255; return c <= 0.03928 ? c/12.92 : ((c+0.055)/1.055)**2.4; }; return 0.2126*f(r)+0.7152*f(g)+0.0722*f(b); };
  const parse = s => { const m = (s.match(/[\d.]+/g)||[]).map(Number); return { rgb: m.slice(0,3), a: m.length > 3 ? m[3] : 1 }; };
  const over = (top, a, under) => top.map((c,i) => Math.round(c*a + under[i]*(1-a)));
  const bgOf = el => { let n = el, stack = []; while (n && n.nodeType === 1) { const { rgb, a } = parse(getComputedStyle(n).backgroundColor); if (a > 0) stack.push({ rgb, a }); if (a >= 1) break; n = n.parentElement; } let out = [255,255,255]; for (let i = stack.length-1; i >= 0; i--) out = over(stack[i].rgb, stack[i].a, out); return out; };
  const ratio = (a, b) => { const x = L(a), y = L(b); return (Math.max(x,y)+0.05)/(Math.min(x,y)+0.05); };
  const root = document.querySelector('.drill-observatory-root') || document.body;
  const walker = document.createTreeWalker(root, NodeFilter.SHOW_TEXT);
  const fails = [], seen = new Set(); let count = 0, node;
  while ((node = walker.nextNode())) {
    const t = node.textContent.trim(); if (!t) continue;
    const el = node.parentElement; if (seen.has(el)) continue; seen.add(el);
    const cs = getComputedStyle(el); if (cs.visibility === 'hidden' || cs.display === 'none') continue;
    const r = el.getBoundingClientRect(); if (r.width === 0 || r.height === 0) continue;
    const fg = parse(cs.color); const fgRgb = fg.a < 1 ? over(fg.rgb, fg.a, bgOf(el)) : fg.rgb;
    const bg = bgOf(el); const c = ratio(fgRgb, bg);
    const size = parseFloat(cs.fontSize), bold = parseInt(cs.fontWeight) >= 700;
    const large = size >= 24 || (size >= 18.66 && bold);
    const need = large ? 3 : 4.5; count++;
    if (c < need) fails.push({ text: t.slice(0,40), ratio: +c.toFixed(2), need, color: cs.color, bg: `rgb(${bg})`, cls: el.className.toString().slice(0,60) });
  }
  return { checked: count, fails };
})()
```

Caveat: text over a photograph reports the scrim colour, not the photo. For the hero (and Today, until WP-A removes its photo), also zoom the screenshot and confirm by eye that the lightest part of the photo under any text still reads. If unsure, darken the scrim.

  return { total: els.length, under44: small };
})()

### WP-E. `!important` to zero inside the observatory block

**Why.** 200 `!important` declarations exist to beat shadcn's Card and Button styles. Theming 5 needs the cascade to work without force.

**Steps.**
1. Understand why they are there: `Card` and `Button` from `src/components/ui/` apply Tailwind utility classes (`rounded-lg border bg-card shadow-sm`, `inline-flex h-10 px-4 ...`). Tailwind utilities have single-class specificity; the observatory rules are also mostly single-class, so the author used `!important` to win. The fix is specificity, not force.
2. Wrap every observatory selector in the block (lines ~840 to ~2060) with the root: `.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-cta { ... }` has (0,2,0) and beats any utility. Do this mechanically with a script, not by hand. Write `scripts/observatory-specificity.mjs` that:
   - reads classroom.css,
   - for every rule between the line containing `.drill-observatory-root {` and the line containing `/* end observatory */` (add that comment marker at the end of the block first, after the reduced-motion rule at ~2055),
   - for each selector in a rule's selector list that starts with `.drill-observatory-` and is not already prefixed, prefixes `.drill-observatory-root `,
   - strips ` !important` from every declaration in that range,
   - writes the file back and prints counts.
   Media queries inside the range are handled the same way (the script only touches selector lines and declaration lines; braces stay).
3. Run the script once. Run typecheck and tests. Publish (with WP-F). Then measure: screenshot at 375 and 1280 and compare to the WP-D screenshots side by side. Anything that changed shape means a rule lost to a utility. Find the rule, raise its specificity by one more class (for example `.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-hero .drill-observatory-cta`), never by `!important`.

### WP-C. The phone menu button stops floating over content

**Why.** On phones the menu button is `position: fixed` at 16px/16px, so once the student scrolls it sits on top of "Your practice" and the Today card. This is the one Responsive defect that is a real usability problem.

**Steps.**
1. In `ClassroomLayout.tsx` around line 1019 the button is rendered as a direct child of the root. Wrap it in a bar: `<div className="classroom-mobile-bar">` containing the button and, to its right, a small text label with the section name (there is an `activeSection` value already in scope; render its display label, or "Drill" on the drill root). Keep the button's `aria-label`.
2. In classroom.css, add:
```css
.classroom-root .classroom-mobile-bar { display: none; }
@media (max-width: 1023px) {
  .classroom-root .classroom-mobile-bar {
    align-items: center;
    background: var(--classroom-page);
    border-bottom: 1px solid var(--classroom-line);
    display: flex;
    gap: 12px;
    height: 56px;
    padding: 0 12px;
    position: sticky;
    top: 0;
    z-index: 70;
  }
  .classroom-root.classroom-root-drill .classroom-mobile-bar { background: var(--classroom-drill-page); }
  .classroom-root .classroom-mobile-menu-button { position: static; }
  .classroom-root .classroom-column { padding-top: 16px; }
  .classroom-root.classroom-root-drill .classroom-stage { padding-top: var(--observatory-space-4); }
}
```

3. The bar is sticky, so it stays at the top while scrolling but is part of the flow. Confirm the jump row starts right under it with no overlap at 375.
4. Every other classroom section (Home, Messages, Schedule, Workspace) shares this shell. Open each on production at 375 after publish and screenshot the top. Nothing may overlap the bar. If a section had its own 72px allowance somewhere else, adjust it.
5. The bar text: mono label style is wrong here (this is the shell, not the hub). Use the shell's own label style, `--classroom-meta-text`, 13px, weight 500.

/* The menu button sits in this bar below 1024px. The bar is sticky, so it
   holds the top of the viewport while the student scrolls, but it takes its
   own 56px in the flow instead of floating over the page the way the fixed
   button did. Above 1024px the sidebar is visible and the bar is not drawn. */
.classroom-root .classroom-mobile-bar {
  display: none;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-mobile-menu-button {
  align-items: center;
  background: var(--classroom-trigger);
  border: 1px solid var(--classroom-line);
  border-radius: var(--classroom-radius);
  cursor: pointer;
  display: none;
  flex: none;
  height: 44px;
  justify-content: center;
  width: 44px;
}

/* The shell's own label voice, not the hub's mono micro-label: this bar is
   part of the classroom chrome and appears on every section. */
.classroom-root .classroom-mobile-bar-label {
  color: var(--classroom-meta-text);
  font-size: 13px;
  font-weight: 500;
  overflow: hidden;
  text-overflow: ellipsis;
  white-space: nowrap;
}

@media (max-width: 1023px) {
  .classroom-root .classroom-sidebar {
    display: none;

  .classroom-root .classroom-mobile-bar {
    align-items: center;
    background: var(--classroom-page);

  }

## assistant

Reset. Here's where things stand.

## `13bb1dd` was already published — and it shipped a real bug

I checked production directly instead of asking: `.classroom-mobile-bar` was present with the menu button at `position: static`, and both datelines rendered. So WP-C and WP-D were live.

But the five-section sweep the plan requires caught a regression. WP-C added the bar as a third child of `.classroom-root` — which is `display: flex` with `align-items: flex-start`, a **row**. Below the sidebar breakpoint the bar claimed its own column instead of a band across the top:

| Surface at 375 | Stage before | Stage after |
|---|---|---|
| Drill hub | `x:104 w:271` (content 239px) | `x:0 w:375` (content 343px) |
| Home | `x:116 w:259` | `x:0 w:375` |
| Schedule | `x:133 w:242` (calendar column 206px) | `x:0 w:375` (339px) |
| Workspace | `x:194 w:181` | `x:0 w:375` |
| Messages | bar 137px wide | bar 375px |

Every scan passed while this was live — contrast 0 fails, targets 0 under 44, `docWidth` 375, nothing past the right edge. Nothing *overflowed*; the room was just squeezed. Only the screenshot showed it, which is exactly why WP-C step 4 asked for one.

Messages exposed a second-order bug in my first fix: in a column the bar is `flex: 0 1 auto`, so a full-height sibling shrank it to 52px. It needs `flex: none`.

## Also finished WP-D, which had not actually met its criterion

At 1024 the *short* dateline still wrapped: the greeting column is 201px and the line measured 197px of glyphs plus 47px of tracking, so "2026" fell to a second line. Tightening tracking on the span holds it to one line (185px), verified at 900, 1024, and 1099. I did that without `!important` — a value set on the element beats one inherited from the label — so the count stayed at 192, never up.

**Pushed `a82333a`. Ready to publish.** Typecheck clean, 98 classroom tests, 25 mainhub tests, `!important` 192, staged `classroom.css` only.

Two things worth recording:

- **A measurement limitation:** the in-app browser returns a blank image for any *scrolled* screenshot of this page (the capture window doesn't follow scroll; content paints fine at `scrollY=150`). I used DOM geometry and `elementFromPoint` for the scrolled overlap proof instead, which is stronger evidence than eyeballing. RUN3 will say so rather than claim a screenshot I don't have.
- **The plan is wrong on one point:** the theme class is `html.dark` ([classroom.css:62](projects/lsatu/src/components/classroom/classroom.css:62)), not the `.light` its Part 0 table claims. WP-F must be written against `html.dark`, as the handoff flagged.

## Why I stopped short of WP-E

I did not start the `!important` rewrite. Publishing a 1,000-line mechanical specificity change *together* with this layout fix would make any visual regression ambiguous — and WP-E's own acceptance test is "looks identical to the WP-D screenshots," which needs a clean published baseline to compare against. Publish `a82333a` first, I'll verify it across the six viewports and the five sections, and that becomes the baseline for E+F.

One decision I'll want your call on when I get there: the `prefers-reduced-motion` block sits inside the observatory range and its `!important`s are load-bearing — an accessibility override that must beat animation declarations, where specificity alone can lose on source order. I plan to keep those and report the count as "zero outside the reduced-motion block" rather than hit a literal zero by weakening a motion-sensitivity guarantee. Say the word if you'd rather I chase the zero.

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Analyze observatory selectors with postcss
command: cat > scripts/_obs_analyze.mjs <<'EOF'
import fs from 'node:fs';
import postcss from 'postcss';
const css = fs.readFileSync('src/components/classroom/classroom.css','utf8');
const root = postcss.parse(css);
let start=-1,end=-1;
root.nodes.forEach((n,i)=>{ if(start<0 && n.type==='rule' && n.selector.trim()==='.drill-observatory-root') start=i; });
root.nodes.forEach((n,i)=>{ if(end<0 && i>start && n.type==='rule' && n.selector.includes('.classroom-dateline')) end=i; });
const nonDrill=new Map(); const rootPrefixed=new Set(); let rules=0, imp=0, impNonDrill=0, impReduced=0;
const visitNode=(node, inReduced)=>{
  if(node.type==='rule'){
    rules++;
    node.selectors.forEach(s=>{
      if(s.startsWith('.drill-observatory-root')) rootPrefixed.add(s);
      else if(!s.startsWith('.drill-observatory-')) nonDrill.set(s,(nonDrill.get(s)||0)+1);
    });
    node.walkDecls(d=>{ if(d.important){ imp++; if(inReduced) impReduced++;
      if(node.selectors.some(s=>!s.startsWith('.drill-observatory-'))) impNonDrill++; } });
  } else if(node.type==='atrule'){
    const red = inReduced || /prefers-reduced-motion/.test(node.params||'');
    node.each(c=>visitNode(c, red));
  }
};
for(let i=start;i<end;i++) visitNode(root.nodes[i], false);
console.log('startIdx',start,'endIdx',end,'rules',rules,'importantDecls',imp,'inReducedMotion',impReduced,'inNonDrillSelectors',impNonDrill);
console.log('--- selectors NOT starting with .drill-observatory- ---');
[...nonDrill.entries()].forEach(([s,c])=>console.log('  ',JSON.stringify(s)));
console.log('--- already .drill-observatory-root* ---');
[...rootPrefixed].forEach(s=>console.log('  ',JSON.stringify(s)));
EOF
node scripts/_obs_analyze.mjs
[/external_agent_tool_call]

description: Precisely audit scoping in the built bundle
command: node -e '
const fs=require("fs"); const postcss=require("postcss");
const css=fs.readFileSync("dist/assets/index-D1D7btRl.css","utf8");
const root=postcss.parse(css);
let scoped=0, unscoped=[], impRules=[];
root.walkRules(rule=>{
  const sels=rule.selectors||[];
  const obs=sels.filter(s=>s.includes(".drill-observatory-"));
  if(!obs.length) return;
  obs.forEach(s=>{
    if(s.startsWith(".drill-observatory-root")) scoped++;
    else unscoped.push(s);
  });
  let hasImp=false; rule.walkDecls(d=>{ if(d.important) hasImp=true; });
  if(hasImp){ let p=rule.parent; const at=[]; while(p&&p.type!=="root"){ if(p.type==="atrule") at.push("@"+p.name+" "+p.params); p=p.parent; }
    impRules.push({sel:sels.join(", ").slice(0,80), at:at.join(" / ")}); }
});
console.log("scoped observatory selector instances:", scoped);
console.log("UNSCOPED observatory selectors:", unscoped.length);
[...new Set(unscoped)].slice(0,20).forEach(s=>console.log("   ", s));
console.log("observatory rules containing !important:", impRules.length);
impRules.forEach(r=>console.log("   ", r.at, "|", r.sel));
'

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Commit WP-E
command: git commit -F - <<'EOF'
Scope the drill hub by specificity instead of 188 !important declarations

The observatory rules compete with shadcn's Card and Button, which style
themselves with Tailwind utilities. A utility is one class, (0,1,0), and so
were most observatory rules, so whichever came last won and the author reached
for !important 192 times. Prefixing a rule with .drill-observatory-root makes
it (0,2,0), which beats any utility on specificity alone and leaves the cascade
working normally for anything that legitimately needs to override.

scripts/observatory-specificity.mjs does the rewrite with a real CSS parser
rather than line surgery, and is idempotent; --check fails if the block drifts
out of scope. 145 rules visited, 159 selectors scoped, 188 !important removed.

Four are kept, all in the prefers-reduced-motion block. That !important is the
switch that turns animation OFF for a reader who asked their OS for less
motion; it has to beat a later, equally specific animation rule, and
specificity cannot promise that. Trading an accessibility guarantee for a
smaller number would be the wrong deal.

Two things are deliberately outside the rewrite. The block now ends at an
explicit /* end observatory */ marker, which keeps

  "product": "# Product\r\n\r\n<!-- impeccable:product-schema 1 -->\r\n\r\n## Scope\r\n\r\nApp-wide only. Every statement in this file must be true of all of LSAT U.\r\n\r\nA rule that binds one surface does not belong here. It goes in that surface's brief under `.impeccable/surfaces/`. This split is deliberate and is the correction to a real failure: the previous PRODUCT.md was written on 2026-05-02 for the Intro to LR bootcamp alone, then widened app-wide on 2026-05-08 in a commit whose stated purpose was adding messenger principles. Bootcamp anti-references silently became binding on the drills, and stayed binding for four months. A one-surface rule written into an app-wide file has no boundary that can hold it. Keeping the file app-wide only removes the mechanism.\r\n\r\nThe repo already carries eight bounded contexts with their own glossaries (`CONTEXT-MAP.md`). Surface-specific product truth follows those boundaries, not this file.\r\n\r\n## Platform\r\n\r\nweb\r\n\r\n## Users\r\n\r\n**Primary: Aspiring Attorneys clients.** Students who are paying Joshua for one-to-one LSAT coaching. They have consultations, scheduled sessions, transcripts, assigned work, and a real back-and-forth with him. The platform is where their coaching lives between sessions. They arrive already knowing who their coach is, and design decisions are made for them first.\r\n\r\n**Secondary: independent self-study learners.** Students using the platform without an active coaching relationship. They are served by the same surfaces but are not the audience a tradeoff is resolved in favour of.\r\n\r\n**Operator: Joshua.** The sole instructor and the product, voice, and content authority. He assigns work, publishes material, reviews student activity, and answers messages. He works from any device at any hour, so operator surfaces are as responsive as student surfaces.\r\n\r\n## Product Purpose\r\n\r\nLSAT U is the operational platform for Joshua's coaching practice. It delivers the method, holds the student's work, and keeps the coaching relationship live between sessions.\r\n\r\nFor a student: one place that knows what they have done, what they got wrong, and what to do next.\r\n\r\nFor Joshua: one workspace to assign, publish, review, and reply without losing the one-to-one feel that his practice is sold on.\r\n\r\nSuccess is a student who logs in without being chased, knows the next action without asking, and can point to what improved.\r\n\r\n## Positioning\r\n\r\n**A real coach is in it.**\r\n\r\nThe claim a competing product cannot truthfully copy is that a named human being is on the other side of the platform, has met this student, and has looked at this student's work. Assignments come from him. Messages are answered by him. The material was authored by him. Nothing in the product should be built in a way that would still be true if the coach were removed, and nothing should imply a coach is present where one is not.\r\n\r\n## Operating Context\r\n\r\nThe platform sits inside a coaching practice that runs partly outside it. Future work must not assume the app is the whole system.\r\n\r\n- Coaching sessions happen on video and are transcribed. Session records, notes, and recaps originate outside the app.\r\n- Booking and payment run through their own flow (packages, booking passes, a public booking microsite, single sessions).\r\n- Students also practice on third-party platforms. Work done elsewhere is real work, and the app is not the only record of a student's effort.\r\n- Joshua authors material ahead of time and assigns it, rather than the system generating it on demand.\r\n- Students use both desktop and mobile, in focused study blocks and in short check-ins.\r\n\r\n## Capabilities and Constraints\r\n\r\nConfirmed surfaces: authentication and onboarding, the foyer, the classroom, guided drills and the question bank, full-passage Reading Comprehension, the Activity Centre, the Testing Centre and practice test runs, ...

> **Additional context needed**: performance constraints.

Analyze a feature and strategically add animations and micro-interactions that enhance understanding, provide feedback, and create delight.

---

## Register

Brand: orchestrated page-load sequences, staggered reveals, scroll-driven animation. Motion is part of the voice; one well-rehearsed entrance beats scattered micro-interactions.

Product: 150–250 ms on most transitions. Motion conveys state — feedback, reveal, loading, transitions between views. No page-load choreography; users are in a task and won't wait for it.

---

## Assess Animation Opportunities

Analyze where motion would improve the experience:

1. **Identify static areas**:
   - **Missing feedback**: Actions without visual acknowledgment (button clicks, form submission, etc.)
   - **Jarring transitions**: Instant state changes that feel abrupt (show/hide, page loads, route changes)
   - **Unclear relationships**: Spatial or hierarchical relationships that aren't obvious
   - **Lack of delight**: Functional but joyless interactions
   - **Missed guidance**: Opportunities to direct attention or explain behavior

2. **Understand the context**:
   - What's the personality? (Playful vs serious, energetic vs calm)
   - What's the performance budget? (Mobile-first? Complex page?)
   - Who's the audience? (Motion-sensitive users? Power users who want speed?)
   - What matters most? (One hero animation vs many micro-interactions?)

If any of these are unclear from the codebase, STOP and use Codex's structured user-input/question tool when available; if unavailable, ask directly in chat to clarify what you cannot infer.

**CRITICAL**: Respect `prefers-reduced-motion`. Always provide non-animated alternatives for users who need them.

## Plan Animation Strategy

Create a purposeful animation plan:

- **Hero moment**: What's the ONE signature animation? (Page load? Hero section? Key interaction?)
- **Feedback layer**: Which interactions need acknowledgment?
- **Transition layer**: Which state changes need smoothing?
- **Delight layer**: Where can we surprise and delight?

**IMPORTANT**: One well-orchestrated experience beats scattered animations everywhere. Focus on high-impact moments.

## Implement Animations

Add motion systematically across these categories:

### Entrance Animations
- **Page load choreography**: Stagger element reveals (100-150ms delays), fade + slide combinations
- **Hero section**: Dramatic entrance for primary content (scale, parallax, or creative effects)
- **Content reveals**: Scroll-triggered animations using intersection observer
- **Modal/drawer entry**: Smooth slide + fade, backdrop fade, focus management

### Micro-interactions
- **Button feedback**:
  - Hover: Subtle scale (1.02-1.05), color shift, shadow increase
  - Click: Quick scale down then up (0.95 → 1), ripple effect
  - Loading: Spinner or pulse state
- **Form interactions**:
  - Input focus: Border color transition, slight scale or glow
  - Validation: Shake on error, check mark on success, smooth color transitions
- **Toggle switches**: Smooth slide + color transition (200-300ms)
- **Checkboxes/radio**: Check mark animation, ripple effect
- **Like/favorite**: Scale + rotation, particle effects, color transition

### State Transitions
- **Show/hide**: Fade + slide (not instant), appropriate timing (200-300ms)
- **Expand/collapse**: Height transition with overflow handling, icon rotation
- **Loading states**: Skeleton screen fades, spinner animations, progress bars
- **Success/error**: Color transitions, icon animations, gentle scale pulse
- **Enable/disable**: Opacity transitions, cursor changes

### Navigation & Flow
- **Page transitions**: Crossfade between routes, shared element transitions
- **Tab switching**: Slide indicator, content fade/slide
- **Carousel/slider**: Smooth transforms, snap points, momentum
- **Scroll effects**: Parallax...

# Surface brief: the drill hub

Route: `/classroom/drill` (also `/practice`, which redirects). Code: `src/pages/AcademyMainhub.tsx`, `src/components/mainhub/`, the `.drill-observatory-*` block of `src/components/classroom/classroom.css`, `src/components/drill/SectionToggle.tsx`. Wrapper: `.classroom-root-drill` and `.drill-observatory-root`.

Written 2026-09-08 from the impeccable audit and critique, after Joshua's two decisions: Today's assigned item leads the hero, and this hub is a sanctioned scoped world with its own record. This file is that record. DESIGN.md's Workroom rules do not apply inside the wrapper; the rules below do.

## What the page is for

A paying client logs in to do what Joshua assigned. The page tells them that first, in the hero, with one button. Everything else on the page (adaptive drill, the builder, the question jump, insights) is the fallback for a student with nothing assigned, or the second thing after the assignment. Product principle 2 (compute the next step, do not present a grid) is the whole layout brief.

Order of the page, top to bottom: utilities (jump, search, LR/RC), the hero (greeting, Next up, one CTA, one quiet alternative, the builder console), the practice card (insights, Analytics, Journal), Today (the queue behind the hero's item).

## The ground

The stage is cream (`--classroom-drill-page`). The cards on it are near-black (`--observatory-surface-card` `#08080b`). One card, the hero, carries a photograph (the horizon), and it is the only decoration on the page. Today was flattened to the same near-black surface as the practice card on 2026-09-08; two photographs on one screen read as a template. No accent colour. No gradients. No grid textures.

Controls that sit on the stage itself (the jump row, the section switch) are inked for the stage: `--observatory-stage-*`. Controls on a card are inked ivory: the `--observatory-ink*` ladder. The 2026-09-08 audit found the jump row inked for the cards while sitting on the stage, at 1.07:1. Tone is decided by what a control sits on, never by what is near it.

## Ink and contrast

WCAG AA is a defect gate here as everywhere. Measured against `#08080b`, the ivory ladder passes for text from `--observatory-ink-50` (4.9:1) upward. The steps below it (0.38, 0.42, 0.45, 0.48) were removed on 2026-09-08; they were carrying labels at 3.2:1 to 4.2:1. Nothing that carries text goes below `ink-50`. Hairlines, rings and skeleton bars use their own tokens and may be fainter.

On the stage: `--observatory-stage-ink` 12.9:1, `--observatory-stage-muted` 5.3:1 on `--observatory-stage-well`.

Over the photographs, text sits on a scrim (`::before` on the hero and on Today's inner surface). The scrim is mandatory; the photographs have bright crops.

## Type

Three families, on purpose, one job each:

- Fraunces, weight 400, `opsz` 60: the greeting only (`h1`). It is the one serif line on the page. Insight values and Today titles use it at 20 to 22px as the page's second display voice. Nothing else.
- JetBrains Mono, 10 to 11px, tracked 0.16 to 0.18em, uppercase: micro-labels and chips. Never a sentence.
- Outfit: everything else. Body 13.5 to 14px, the Next up title 19px semibold, the console heading fluid.

## Depth

Hairline rings (`0 0 0 1px`) separate surfaces. Shadows are quiet: `0 12px 32px` at 12 to 16 percent, not the 60px at 25 to 35 percent the page shipped with. Glass appears only on the hero console. It is the one `backdrop-filter` on the page, at `blur(12px)`, and it is there because it sits over the photograph. A glass panel anywhere else, including on Today, is a defect.

## Controls

- 44px minimum by height, met by the control: the CTA (46px), the console button (46px), the section switch options (44px), selects (44px), secondary links and insight actions (44px). No pseudo-element hit areas.
- Focus: a 2px solid ivory outline (`--observatory-focus`), offset 3px, on every pill control. Full-bleed rows inside a clipped container use the ins...
[/external_agent_tool_result]

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Audit hover/active properties against transition lists
command: cat > scripts/_motion_audit.mjs <<'EOF'
import fs from 'node:fs'; import postcss from 'postcss';
const css=fs.readFileSync('src/components/classroom/classroom.css','utf8');
const ast=postcss.parse(css);
const start=ast.nodes.findIndex(n=>n.type==='rule'&&n.selector.trim()==='.drill-observatory-root');
const end=ast.nodes.findIndex(n=>n.type==='comment'&&n.text.trim()==='end observatory');
const base=new Map();   // base selector -> transition property list
const states=new Map(); // base selector -> Set(props changed on hover/active)
const norm=s=>s.replace(/:(hover|active|focus-visible|focus|disabled|not\([^)]*\))/g,'').replace(/\s+/g,' ').trim();
const visit=n=>{
  if(n.type==='rule'){
    n.selectors.forEach(sel=>{
      const b=norm(sel);
      const isState=/:(hover|active)/.test(sel);
      n.walkDecls(d=>{
        if(d.prop==='transition'||d.prop==='transition-property'){ if(!isState) base.set(b,d.value); return; }
        if(isState){ if(!states.has(b)) states.set(b,new Set()); states.get(b).add(d.prop); }
      });
    });
  } else if(n.type==='atrule'){ n.each(visit); }
};
for(let i=start;i<end;i++) visit(ast.nodes[i]);
const expand=p=>p==='background'?['background-color','background-image']:p==='border'?['border-color']:[p];
console.log('--- state changes NOT covered by the base transition ---');
let gaps=0;
for(const [sel,props] of states){
  const tr=base.get(sel);
  const covered=tr?tr:'(no transition declared)';
  const missing=[...props].flatMap(expand).filter(p=>{
    if(!tr) return true;
    if(p==='transform'&&/transform/.test(tr)) return false;
    if(p==='filter'&&/filter/.test(tr)) return false;
    const key=p.replace('-color','');
    return !(new RegExp(key).test(tr));
  });
  if(missing.length){ gaps++; console.log(`  ${sel}\n      changes: ${[...props].join(', ')}\n      transition: ${covered}\n      NOT ANIMATED: ${[...new Set(missing)].join(', ')}`); }
}
console.log('gaps:', gaps);
EOF
node scripts/_motion_audit.mjs
[/external_agent_tool_call]

description: Commit the motion pass
command: git commit -F - <<'EOF'
Give every control on the hub a pointer answer, and an ease-out curve

Two measured defects, not decoration.

The curve. --observatory-motion used cubic-bezier(0.4, 0, 0.2, 1), an
ease-in-out. A curve that eases in spends its opening barely moving, which is
right for something leaving the screen and wrong for a control answering a
pointer: at 10% of a 160ms transition it has covered 2.6% of the distance and
at 25% only 23.7%, so nothing visible happens for the first 40ms and the hub
reads as slow. The quart ease-out covers 34.7% and 68.9% at those points. It
is now --observatory-ease, used by the state token and the stage entrance.

The jump row. The three selects are the first controls a student meets on the
hub and had no hover state at all: :disabled and :focus-visible existed, and
nothing answered a pointer, against the surface brief's own rule. They now
strengthen their ring, mixed from --qf-ink so it reads in both the light row
and the dark tone. The reduced-motion block gains .qj: the row was covered
only because it happens to sit inside .drill-observatory-root, which is
incidental and would fall away if the row were used anywhere else.

Three transition lists also gained background-color, because their :hover
changes it and the list named only transform. That was masked until now by a
Tailwind utility outranking the rule; once the previous commit let the rules
win on specificity, those hovers would have snapped instead of blending. The
CTA, the practice tile and the insight action.

Split commit, worth knowing: the classroom.css half of this change was swept
into 70056d3 by a concurrent session's broad git add, so that commit carries
motion work its message does not mention.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>
EOF

## assistant

Viewport reset. Here's where things stand.

## First, the thing I said badly

Plainly: browsers have a "reduce motion" accessibility setting for people who get motion sick. The hub has a rule that switches all its animation off when that setting is on. That off-switch is written with `!important` — CSS's "force this" keyword.

The plan told me to remove every `!important` from the hub. Removing those four would mean the off-switch can lose to an animation rule that comes later in the file, so someone who asked their computer for less motion might still get it.

So I removed 188 and kept 4. The count went **192 → 4**, and the 4 that remain are only that off-switch. That was the decision I was asking you to confirm, and I've now made it that way.

## The push rule — you're right, and I'd already broken it

My push went out as `e1b2507..5fd1074`, which carried another session's commit `70056d3` along with mine. Saved as memory: check `git log origin/main..HEAD` before pushing and stop if anything there isn't mine.

Related, and you should know it: that same session's broad `git add` swept **my** uncommitted motion edits into *its* commit `70056d3`, so that commit contains motion work its message doesn't mention. I couldn't unpick it without rewriting their commit, so I said so in mine instead. There's now another of their commits, `292e343`, sitting unpushed — under the new rule I've left it alone.

## The motion work: two measured defects, no decoration

I checked before adding anything, and most of what I expected to be missing already existed — the section switch is a proper framer-motion spring, both skeletons already pulse, there are 14 hovers and 6 press states. So this was a correction pass.

**The easing curve was the wrong family.** The hub used `cubic-bezier(0.4, 0, 0.2, 1)`, an ease-in-out. A curve that eases *in* barely moves at the start, which is right for something leaving the screen and wrong for a control answering a pointer. Measured on both curves:

| Elapsed | Old curve | Ease-out quart |
|---|---|---|
| 10% (16ms) | 2.6% | 34.7% |
| 25% (40ms) | 23.7% | 68.9% |

Nothing visible happened for the first ~40ms of every hover. That's why it read slow.

**The three jump-row selects had no hover state at all** — `:disabled` and `:focus-visible` existed, nothing answered a pointer, against the brief's own rule. They now strengthen their ring, mixed from the ink token so it reads in both the light row and the dark tone.

**And WP-E would have broken three hovers.** The CTA, practice tile and insight action change `background` on hover, but their transition lists named only `transform`. That was masked because a Tailwind utility outranked the rule — the moment WP-E let the authored rules win on specificity, those three hovers would have snapped. Caught by auditing every hover against its transition list, before anyone saw it.

Verified: `color-mix` resolves to a real oklab value in-browser, the nested `var()` motion token substitutes, the select's transition computes to `box-shadow 0.16s cubic-bezier(0.25, 1, 0.5, 1)`. I could not get the harness to produce a real CSS `:hover`, so I verified that chain through the CSSOM instead — rule parses, element matches everything but `:hover`, value resolves — rather than claiming a hover I didn't see.

Typecheck clean, 17 files / 179 tests passing, production build succeeds, `!important` 4, `--check` confirms the block stays scoped.

## What I need from you

**Publish.** Pushed and waiting: `3eb6803` (WP-E) and `5fd1074` (motion).

When you confirm, I run the check I set up for it: before pushing WP-E I captured a computed-style fingerprint of the live published hub — 200 elements × 50 properties at 375 and 1280, stored in the browser. WP-E is supposed to change *nothing* visually, so I diff against that and any element that moved shows up by name. That's the "looks identical" test the plan asks for, done numerically, since this pane can't screenshot a scrolled page.

Then WP-F is next, written against `html.dark` — the plan's claim that the theme class is `.light` is wrong.

## assistant

All 136 box diffs are the same artifact: my baseline was captured mid-`classroom-stage-enter`, so 119 boxes are exactly 4px up and the other 17 add sub-pixel reflow. The real changes are three groups, and they're all the authored rules finally winning:

- **fontFamily ×5** — section toggle Outfit → JetBrains Mono (the brief says chips are mono; a utility was beating the rule)
- **color ×9 + borderTopColor ×2** — `rgb(18,18,18)` → ivory on the hero card and console (shadcn's `text-card-foreground` was winning on a near-black card)
- **width ×6, marginLeft, gap 8→9px** — reflow from the font change and the CTA's own gap winning

The color flip is the one that could hurt, so let me prove nothing became unreadable.

## assistant

Now I have the real diagnosis, and the plan's WP-F is built on a false premise:

- `:root` = dark palette; `.light` class = light palette; default is light
- Dark is signalled by the **absence** of `.light`
- classroom.css keys its entire dark palette on `html.dark` — which nothing in the app ever sets

So the classroom's dark palette is dead code. Switch to dark and the app chrome goes dark while the whole classroom stays cream. Adding dark stage tokens under `html.dark`, as WP-F instructs, would be dead on arrival too.

Let me test whether the already-authored dark palette actually holds up, by simulating the target state on production.

### WP-G. Fewer labels, less text-shadow, quieter hero

**Why.** Anti-patterns and Aesthetic. The mono uppercase micro-label is used many times on one screen; each one is a decoration when it is not carrying a fact. Text-shadow on every hero line is doing the scrim's job.

**Steps.**
1. Count `drill-observatory-label` usages rendered on the default LR stage (search the three TSX files). List them in the report. Keep: the "Next up" facts line, the date, the "Your practice" and "Also today" card headings, the insight kicker. Remove: any label that repeats the heading above it, and any label on a control (a button says what it does in sentence case).
2. Hero text-shadows: with the scrim doing its job, reduce `text-shadow` on `.drill-observatory-greeting h1` to `0 1px 2px rgba(0,0,0,0.35)` and on `.drill-observatory-greeting p`, `.drill-observatory-next-title`, `.drill-observatory-quiet-link` to `0 1px 2px rgba(0,0,0,0.3)`. Then re-run 1a and check by eye at the lightest crop of the photo. If any hero text is hard to read, darken the scrim by 0.05 alpha rather than restoring the shadow.
3. The quiet link underline: `text-decoration-color: rgba(244, 241, 234, 0.55)` (was 0.35).
4. The CTA's `box-shadow: 0 8px 32px rgba(0,0,0,0.4)` (classroom.css ~1249): reduce to `0 6px 20px rgba(0,0,0,0.3)`.
5. Screenshot the hero at 1280 before and after; put both in the report.

**Done when.** Label count on the LR default stage is at most 6, shadows reduced, contrast still 0 fails.

### WP-H. Copy pass in the teaching voice

**Why.** Critique 2 is about the student's words, not the product's. Read `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and `wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md` first.

**Steps.**
1. List every string a student sees on the hub (hero, console, practice, Today, jump row, search trigger, section switch, loading and error states). Put the list in the report with a column for the new text.
2. Rules for the new text: sentence case on buttons and headings; the verb first on buttons; say what the thing does before naming it; no "Smart" as a bare adjective without the sentence explaining it; no "instead" links that assume the student rejected the first option.
3. Specific changes:
   - Quiet link "Start a Smart Drill instead" becomes "Or practice your weakest types". RC: "Or read one passage now".
   - Fallback CTA "Start Smart Drill" becomes "Practice your weakest types". RC "Start One Passage" becomes "Read one passage".
   - Fallback paragraph: "Start with the question types you miss most, or build a drill around exactly what you want to practice." RC: "Read one passage now, untimed, with the answers after it. Or build a set of passages around what you want to read."
   - "Set up an LR drill" stays. "Set up an RC drill" stays.
   - "Analytics" and "Journal" links stay.
   - Insight action labels: check each in `insightSlides.ts` against rule 2. Rewrite any that names a screen instead of an outcome ("Open analytics" becomes "See where you lose points").
   - Today "Also today" stays. Its empty line stays.
   - Jump row placeholder stays ("PT101.S2.Q1" form).
4. The heading in the console "Build the drill you need." stays; it is the one door and it says what it does.
5. Update tests that assert the old strings (`AcademyMainhub.test.tsx` looks for "Start Smart Drill" and similar; search the three test files for each old string).
6. Add the new strings to `wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md` under a dated heading so future work does not revert them.

**Done when.** Every string in the list has been checked, tests updated and green, the corrections log has the entry.

input: {"action":"javascript_exec","text":"const img=new Image(); img.crossOrigin='anonymous'; img.src='https://lsatprep.study/mainhub/hub-horizon.webp';\nawait img.decode();\nconst HW=343, HH=477;                     // hero box\nconst scale=Math.max(HW/img.width, HH/img.height);   // cover\nconst dw=img.width*scale, dh=img.height*scale;\nconst offX=(HW-dw)*0.50, offY=(HH-dh)*0.78;          // background-position 50% 78%\nconst c=document.createElement('canvas'); c.width=HW; c.height=HH;\nconst ctx=c.getContext('2d',{willReadFrequently:true});\nctx.fillStyle='rgb(5,5,7)'; ctx.fillRect(0,0,HW,HH);\nctx.drawImage(img, offX, offY, dw, dh);\n// link box relative to hero\nconst x0=144, x1=308, y0=195, y1=239;\nconst d=ctx.getImageData(x0,y0,x1-x0,y1-y0).data;\nconst lum=([r,g,b])=>{const f=v=>{v/=255;return v<=0.03928?v/12.92:((v+0.055)/1.055)**2.4;};return 0.2126*f(r)+0.7152*f(g)+0.0722*f(b);};\nconst over=(top,a,und)=>top.map((ch,i)=>ch*a+und[i]*(1-a));\n// horizontal scrim alpha at a given relX\nconst scrimA=rx=>rx<=0.45 ? 0.82+(0.35-0.82)*(rx/0.45) : 0.35+(0.70-0.35)*((rx-0.45)/0.55);\nlet worst={ratio:99};\nfor(let py=0;py<y1-y0;py++){\n  for(let px=0;px<x1-x0;px++){\n    const i=(py*(x1-x0)+px)*4;\n    const photo=[d[i],d[i+1],d[i+2]];\n    const rx=(x0+px)/HW;\n    const bg=over([4,4,6], scrimA(rx), photo);        // scrim over photo\n    const fg=over([244,241,234], 0.7, bg);            // text is 70% opaque ivory\n    const L1=lum(fg), L2=lum(bg);\n    const ratio=(Math.max(L1,L2)+0.05)/(Math.min(L1,L2)+0.05);\n    if(ratio<worst.ratio) worst={ratio:+ratio.toFixed(2), photo, scrimAlpha:+scrimA(rx).toFixed(3), bg:bg.map(v=>Math.round(v)), fg:fg.map(v=>Math.round(v)), atX:x0+px, atY:y0+py};\n  }\n}\n({ note:'worst-case pixel under the quiet link, photo+scrim composited, text at its real 0.7 alpha',\n   worstContrast: worst.ratio, needsForSmallText: 4.5, worst })"}

description: Commit WP-G
command: git add src/components/classroom/classroom.css src/components/mainhub/TodayFocusPanel.tsx .impeccable/surfaces/drill-hub.md && git diff --cached --name-only && git commit -F - <<'EOF'
Fix three hero contrast failures the scan could not see, and quiet the shadows

The contrast scan reads the scrim's declared colour, not the photograph under
it, so it has been reporting zero fails on a hero where three lines were
failing. Sampling hub-horizon.webp under each text box and compositing photo,
scrim and the text's own alpha: the quiet link measured 1.89:1, the Next up
facts line 2.76:1 and the dateline 4.50:1, all needing 4.5. The horizon crop
reaches rgb(250, 240, 228) and the horizontal scrim thins to 0.35 exactly
there.

Two changes, together, because neither is enough alone. The ink ladder's alpha
steps were measured against the flat card #08080b and do not survive a
photograph, so hero text that carries meaning is full ivory; and the scrim's
middle stop goes 0.35 to 0.58. 0.58 is the measured floor, not a guess: it is
the smallest value that clears every hero line once the text is full ivory,
and it still opens the middle enough for the horizon to read. Worst line is
now the quiet link at 4.99, the dateline 12.62, the facts line 7.75.

Found while doing WP-G's shadow reduction, which made it visible: the hero
text-shadows drop from 24px/12px at 60% to 2px at 30-35%, and that shadow had
been quietly propping up text the scrim should have been carrying. The CTA's
drop shadow goes from 0 8px 32px at 40% to 0 6px 20px at 30%, and the quiet
link's underline from 0.35 to 0.55, since the underline is the only thing
marking it as a link.

Also WP-G's label cull. Today printed the date a second time, three lines
under the hero's own dateline and in the same mono treatment; it is gone. The
Ready / New chips inside the Today rails were mono uppercase micro-labels on a
control, and the label voice is for facts on a surface: they are sentence case
now, which is what the data always said.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>
EOF
git log --oneline -1
[/external_agent_tool_call]

## Words never to use in student copy

From the glossary. Internal names may stay in code, never on the page.

| Never | Use instead |
|---|---|
| beat, beats | step |
| chunk | piece |
| intake | plain instruction |
| briefing, phase, tab, card | the thing's own name |
| say it back | tell it back |
| The Read | The Recall |
| Recall Rephrase Colour | Read, Recall, Colour |
| em dash, en dash | a full stop, or a colon before a list |

Related: [[teaching-voice]] · [[ASSIGN-AN-ACTIVITY]] · [[STUDENT-PLATFORM-IDENTITY-MAP]]
[/external_agent_tool_result]

  const primary = !focus.loading && !focus.hasError ? focus.primary : null;

  return (
    /* Surface, scrim and grid live in .drill-observatory-hero / -hero-grid: the

              <div className="drill-observatory-next animate-pulse" aria-label="Loading today">
                <div className="drill-observatory-skeleton-bar h-2.5 w-28" />

          <div className="drill-observatory-cta-row">

# Copy corrections

Every student-facing wording fix, with the reason it was wrong. Read this before
writing or editing copy for LSAT U so the same mistake is not made twice.

Companion to [[teaching-voice]], which says how the writing should sound. This
says what has actually gone wrong and why.

Started 2026-09-07 from a Read, Recall, Colour pass. Add to it every time a
correction is made.

## The rules these corrections came from

Three rules already existed and all three leaked anyway, because each was
enforced on the components that happened to exist when it was written. A surface
added later starts uncovered.

- **No em dashes, ever.** [[teaching-voice]], Core voice.
[/external_agent_tool_result]

## Booking emails: hand over the link, not the pass settings (2026-09-08)

**Cut:** "70 minutes each, and no expiration date, so there's no rush to use them."
**Keep:** "You're set up on LSAT U with 10 sessions."

Session length and expiry are admin fields. The student sees them on the booking
page when she picks a time, so putting them in the email is detail she did not ask
for and has to read past to reach the link. The credit count is worth saying because
it tells her how much she has. Everything else about the pass stays in the backend.

**Lesson:** in a booking email, say who it's for, how many sessions, and the link.
Stop there.

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_result]
/**
 * Which section of the drill hub a student is looking at.
 *
 * The hub is one page with two sections, LR and RC, chosen by the toggle in
 * its tools row. Section is URL state (`?section=rc`), not component state, so
 * a refresh, a Back press, or a shared link all land where the student was.
 * The last section is remembered per browser and applied ONLY when the URL is
 * silent — a deep link always outranks the remembered value.
 */
export type DrillSection = "lr" | "rc";

export const DRILL_SECTION_PARAM = "section";
export const DRILL_SECTION_STORAGE_KEY = "lsatu.drill.section";

/** `rc` and nothing else. Anything unrecognised is LR, never an error. */
export function parseDrillSection(value: string | null | undefined): DrillSection | null {
  if (value == null) return null;
  const v = value.trim().toLowerCase();
  if (v === "rc") return "rc";
  if (v === "lr") return "lr";
  return null;
}

/**
 * Storage is wrapped because it throws outright in Safari private mode and in
 * a sandboxed iframe — a remembered preference is never worth a blank page.
 */
export function readRememberedSection(): DrillSection | null {
  try {
    return parseDrillSection(window.localStorage.getItem(DRILL_SECTION_STORAGE_KEY));
  } catch {
    return null;
  }
}

export function rememberSection(section: DrillSection): void {
  try {
    window.localStorage.setItem(DRILL_SECTION_STORAGE_KEY, section);
  } catch {
    /* no-op: see readRememberedSection */
  }
}

/**
 * The one resolution rule: an explicit `?section=` wins, then the remembered
 * value, then LR. A student who has never chosen lands on LR every time — the
 * toggle is the discovery, and the page never moves under them.
 *
 * "Present but unrecognised" is NOT the same as absent. A link that says
 * `?section=sideways` has stated a section, badly; honouring the browser's
 * remembered RC there would open a section the link did not ask for. Any
 * stated section that is not RC resolves to LR, which is what the spec says
 * and what a malformed link should get.
 */
export function resolveDrillSection(param: string | null | undefined): DrillSection {
  const stated = param != null && param.trim() !== "";
  if (stated) return parseDrillSection(param) ?? "lr";
  return readRememberedSection() ?? "lr";
}

export const DRILL_SECTION_LABELS: Record<DrillSection, { short: string; full: string; route: string }> = {
  lr: { short: "LR", full: "Logical Reasoning", route: "DRILL / LOGICAL REASONING" },
  rc: { short: "RC", full: "Reading Comprehension", route: "DRILL / READING COMPREHENSION" },
};
[/external_agent_tool_result]

export default function QuestionFinder({ returnTo, tone = "light", bank }: QuestionFinderProps) {
  const [open, setOpen] = React.useState(false);

  /* Cmd/Ctrl+K is an accelerator on top of a visible, labelled control — never
     the only way in. It is ignored while the student is typing somewhere else,
     so it cannot steal a keystroke from a real field. */
  React.useEffect(() => {
    const onKey = (event: KeyboardEvent) => {
      if (event.key.toLowerCase() !== "k" || !(event.metaKey || event.ctrlKey)) return;
      const target = event.target as HTMLElement | null;
      const tag = target?.tagName;
      if (!open && (tag === "INPUT" || tag === "TEXTAREA" || target?.isContentEditable)) return;
      event.preventDefault();
      setOpen((value) => !value);
    };
    window.addEventListener("keydown", onKey);
    return () => window.removeEventListener("keydown", onKey);
  }, [open]);

  return (
    <div className="qf" data-tone={tone}>
      {/* ONE trigger, not one per breakpoint. Two buttons behind a CSS
          display swap would both stay in the accessibility tree, and a screen
          reader would announce "Find a question" twice on every visit. The
          label is what collapses on a phone; the control and its accessible
          name do not change. The shortcut still works but is not advertised
          on the trigger: Joshua's call, 2026-09-08. */}
      <button
        type="button"
        className="qf-trigger"

function renderHub(entry: string) {
  const queryClient = new QueryClient({ defaultOptions: { queries: { retry: false } } });
  return render(
    <QueryClientProvider client={queryClient}>
      <MemoryRouter
        initialEntries={[entry]}
        future={{ v7_relativeSplatPath: true, v7_startTransition: true }}
      >
        <AcademyMainhub />
        <UrlProbe />
        <BackButton />
      </MemoryRouter>
    </QueryClientProvider>,
  );
}

const url = () => new URLSearchParams(screen.getByTestId("url").textContent ?? "");
const lrSwitch = () => screen.getByRole("button", { name: "Logical Reasoning" });
const rcSwitch = () => screen.getByRole("button", { name: "Reading Comprehension" });

describe("AcademyMainhub — the LR/RC section switch", () => {
  beforeEach(() => {
    window.HTMLElement.prototype.scrollIntoView = vi.fn();
    window.localStorage.clear();
  });
=== tail ===

  it("keeps section choice in the LR/RC switch instead of repeating it as a practice tile", () => {
    renderHub("/classroom/drill");
    expect(
      screen.queryByRole("button", {
        name: "Reading Comprehension Drill full passages the way they appear on the test.",
      }),
    ).toBeNull();

    fireEvent.click(rcSwitch());
    expect(url().get("section")).toBe("rc");
    expect(screen.getByRole("region", { name: "Practice and insight workspace" })).toBeInTheDocument();
  });
});

command: SCRATCH="/c/Users/Joshu/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/810e4352-f17a-4f97-96c4-7e4935eb07bb/scratchpad"
cat > "$SCRATCH/esctest.txt" <<'TESTS'

describe("AcademyMainhub — Escape leaves a stage", () => {
  beforeEach(() => {
    window.HTMLElement.prototype.scrollIntoView = vi.fn();
    window.localStorage.clear();
  });

  it("closes an open stage and returns the hub", () => {
    renderHub("/classroom/drill?stage=type-drill");
    expect(screen.getByRole("region", { name: "Unified LR drill builder" })).toBeInTheDocument();

    fireEvent.keyDown(window, { key: "Escape" });

    expect(url().get("stage")).toBeNull();
    expect(screen.queryByRole("region", { name: "Unified LR drill builder" })).not.toBeInTheDocument();
    expect(screen.getByRole("region", { name: "Practice and insight workspace" })).toBeInTheDocument();
  });

  it("does nothing on the plain hub, where there is no room to leave", () => {
    renderHub("/classroom/drill?section=rc");
    fireEvent.keyDown(window, { key: "Escape" });

    expect(url().get("stage")).toBeNull();
    expect(url().get("section")).toBe("rc");
    expect(screen.getByRole("region", { name: "Practice and insight workspace" })).toBeInTheDocument();
  });

  it("leaves the stage alone while something modal is open", () => {
    // A dialog closes itself on Escape. Closing the stage underneath it would
    // take two rooms away for one keypress.
    renderHub("/classroom/drill?stage=type-drill");
    const dialog = document.createElement("div");
    dialog.setAttribute("role", "dialog");
    document.body.appendChild(dialog);

    fireEvent.keyDown(window, { key: "Escape" });

    expect(url().get("stage")).toBe("type-drill");
    expect(screen.getByRole("region", { name: "Unified LR drill builder" })).toBeInTheDocument();
    dialog.remove();
  });
});

description: Commit WP-H and WP-I
command: git add src/pages/AcademyMainhub.tsx src/pages/AcademyMainhub.test.tsx src/pages/AcademyMainhub.section.test.tsx src/components/mainhub/insightSlides.ts src/components/questionfinder/QuestionFinder.tsx .impeccable/surfaces/drill-hub.md && git diff --cached --name-only && git commit -F - <<'EOF'
Buttons say the outcome, and Escape leaves a stage

Copy first. The hub was naming its own features on its controls. "Smart Drill"
is the product's word for the adaptive set, and on a button it asks the student
to guess what she is about to get; the sentence above the button still names
it, which is where a product name belongs. So the button says "Practice your
weakest types", the RC one "Read one passage", and the insight actions say the
outcome instead of the screen: "See where you lose points" for Open analytics,
"Review your missed questions" for Open journal.

The quiet second option said "instead", which tells a student she has declined
the assigned work by reading past it. She has not. "Or" offers without
narrating a decision she never made. The fallback paragraph drops "an adaptive
set that follows your weakest question types" for "the question types you miss
most": the same fact without the product vocabulary. Reasons and the full table
are in wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md under 2026-09-09.

Escape now leaves an open stage, which is what every other room in this app
does. It is deliberately narrow: only when a stage is open, never while
something modal is up (that dialog closes itself, and taking the stage away
underneath it would cost two rooms for one keypress), and never out of a field
the student has typed into, where Escape is the browser's own revert. Cancel is
still the visible way out. Three tests cover it, including the modal guard.

The search gains "/" alongside Cmd/Ctrl+K, in the same handler, opening only:
"/" with the panel already open is a character someone is trying to type. The
trigger declares both in aria-keyshortcuts and a title rather than drawing a
badge, which Joshua removed on 2026-09-08.

Two things WP-I asked for were already true and are now recorded rather than
rebuilt: opening a stage pushes a history entry, so Back already returned to
the hub, and section memory already lived in lsatu.drill.section behind a
try/catch, with a stated ?section= outranking it.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
EOF

function isActiveStatus(status: string) {
  return status === "assigned" || status === "in_progress" || status === "viewed" || status === "read" || status === "started";
}

function classroomItemToFocus(item: ClassroomItem): TodayFocusItem | null {
  if (!isActiveStatus(item.status)) return null;

A failed hero stops pretending nothing is assigned, and one explainer arrives

The hub read a failed load and an empty list the same way. primary is null in
both cases, so a student whose assignments did not load was told, silently,
that she had none. The hero now names the failure before it offers her own
practice, and the Today card does the same for its queue. Both carry a Try
again.

That button reloads. Today is assembled from seven sources and only two are
react-query backed, so invalidating queries would retry two of the seven while
looking like it had retried all of them. A reload retries all seven and is the
honest action until those hooks are converted. Today's old copy told the
student to refresh the page herself; the button does the work now.

One explainer, a details under the fallback paragraph, for the student with
nothing assigned: nothing on this page said what "adaptive" meant, and she is
the one being asked to trust it. It quotes no set size. The plan expected ten;
adaptive mode passes questionBank.getAllQuestions() and serves one at a time,
so there is no fixed count and inventing one would be worse than omitting it.

One line under a leading assignment, the first three times only, saying that
assigned work comes first and her own practice is below. After three it has
done its job and a permanent caption would be furniture.

Icons on the default stage were four sizes and four stroke weights; the inline
ones are now 14px at stroke 2. The two left alone are deliberate: the practice
tile is off the default stage, and the chat dock is separate chrome.

Two things WP-K asked for turned out not to need code. The jump row already
disables each select until its predecessor is chosen and puts the reason in the
placeholder. The hero CTA's disabled state is unreachable: resolveItemRoute
returns a string on every branch and notifications always carry onOpen, so
writing a reason sentence for it would be dead copy for a state that cannot
happen. The guard stays as a net.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
EOF

): TodayFocusItem | null {
  if (!row) return null;
  const snapshot = row.state_snapshot;
  const target = resolveResumeTarget(snapshot);
  const label = labelFor(snapshot);
  if (snapshot.kind === "bootcamp_step") {
    return {
      id: `last-${row.activity_id}`,
      type: "bootcamp",
      title: label,
      sourceLine: "Bootcamp progress",
      status: "In progress",
      actionLabel: "Continue",
      route: target.path,
      routeState: target.state,
      priority: 520,
      updatedAt: row.updated_at,
      Icon: BookOpenCheck,
    };
  }

  const total = snapshot.qids.length;
  const left = Math.max(0, total - snapshot.current_index);
  const progress = total > 0 ? `${Math.min(100, Math.round((snapshot.current_index / total) * 100))}%` : undefined;
  return {
    id: `last-${row.activity_id}`,
    type: snapshot.kind === "section" ? "set" : "drill",
    title: label,
    sourceLine: snapshot.kind === "section" ? "Active practice set" : "Unfinished drill",
    status: "In progress",
    actionLabel: "Resume",
    actionCue: left > 0 ? `${left} left` : undefined,
    route: target.path,
    routeState:
      target.path === "/drill"
        ? { ...(target.state as object), returnTo: drillReturnTo }
        : target.state,
    priority: snapshot.kind === "section" ? 480 : 460,
    progress,

Everything is pushed and the report is written. Here's where the job landed.

## Done and published (verified on production)

**WP-A/B, C, D, E, motion.** The two that mattered most:

`13bb1dd` shipped a regression that **every scan passed**. WP-C added the phone menu bar as a third child of `.classroom-root`, which is a flex *row* — so the bar took a column of its own and squeezed every classroom section. Workspace was down to 181px of 375. Nothing overflowed, so contrast, targets, overflow and heading scans all came back clean; only the screenshot sweep caught it. Fixed in `a82333a` and re-verified across all five sections.

**WP-E took `!important` from 200 to 4**, verified in the built bundle rather than just the source. The four that remain are the `prefers-reduced-motion` block — that `!important` is the switch that turns animation off for someone who asked their OS for less motion, and it has to beat a later animation rule. I did not trade that for a round number.

Since screenshots of a scrolled page come back blank in this pane, I proved "the page looks identical" with a 200-element × 50-property fingerprint diffed before and after publish. It changed exactly three things, all of them the authored rules finally beating Tailwind utilities.

## Done and pushed, not yet published

`afb15a3`, `6109c2f`, `daa41d8` — WP-G, H, I, J, K, L, M, N. **Publish when you can; none of it is measured on the live build yet.**

The find worth your attention: **the contrast scan has been reporting zero fails while three hero lines were failing.** It reads the scrim's declared colour, not the photograph under it. Sampling the actual webp pixel-by-pixel under each text box:

| | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| "Start a Smart Drill instead" | **1.89:1** | 4.99 |
| Next up facts line | **2.76:1** | 7.75 |
| Dateline | **4.50:1** | 12.62 |

The ink ladder's alpha steps were measured against the flat card, and they don't survive a photograph. Hero text is full ivory now and the scrim's middle stop went 0.35 → 0.58, which is the measured floor, not a guess.

## Four places the plan was wrong

I verified each before setting it aside rather than implementing it blindly.

**WP-F I did not ship.** Its premise is false: `ThemeContext` adds `.light` and removes it, so dark is the *absence* of a class, while `classroom.css` keys its whole dark palette on `html.dark` — which nothing in the app ever sets. Twenty-two dark rules are dead code. Adding dark stage tokens under `html.dark`, as WP-F instructs, would have been dead on arrival too. I simulated the real fix on production to confirm the diagnosis. But the correct fix changes the selector for the entire classroom, all five sections, for every student in dark theme, and it has never once been rendered. That is its own session with its own verification pass, not a line item inside a drill-hub polish plan. **That's your call to make, not mine to slip in.**

The others: WP-K's disabled-CTA "trap" is an unreachable state (`resolveItemRoute` returns a string on every branch); WP-J's "define a scale" was already done at `classroom.css:954` and its proposed values would have moved every size on the page; and the adaptive drill has no fixed ten-question set, so the explainer quotes no number.

## Scores

Audit **16/20 → 21/25**, critique **31/40 → 42/50** (80%→84% and 78%→84% like-for-like). Every row has evidence tied to a measurement; Performance stays at **3** because post-preload LCP could not be measured and a 3 without evidence stays a 3.

## Two things I owe you plainly

**I broke your push rule before you gave it.** My earlier push went out as `e1b2507..5fd1074`, carrying another session's commit `70056d3` with mine. That same session's broad `git add` had also swept my motion edits into *their* commit, so `70056d3` contains work its message doesn't mention. ## Decisions locked + what shipped
- **WP-E: `!important` 200 → 4**, via `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu\scripts\observatory-specificity.mjs` (postcss, idempotent, `--check` guard). Four kept in `prefers-reduced-motion` deliberately: that `!important` is the off-switch for motion-sensitive users and must beat a later animation rule. Verified in the built bundle, not just source.
- **`.drill-observatory-launcher-panel` deliberately excluded** via a `/* end observatory */` marker — it lands on Radix portal content outside the root; scoping it would unstyle every dropdown.
- **WP-C regression found and fixed (`a82333a`).** `13bb1dd` added the phone bar as a third child of `.classroom-root`, a flex *row*, squeezing all five classroom sections (Workspace to 181px of 375). Every scan passed; only the screenshot sweep caught it. Bar needs `flex: none` (Messages shrank it to 52px).
- **Three hero WCAG failures found that the 1a scan structurally cannot see** — it reads the scrim's declared colour, not the photograph. Measured by canvas-sampling `hub-horizon.webp`: quiet link 1.89:1, Next up facts 2.76:1, dateline 4.50:1. Fixed by full-ivory hero text plus scrim mid-stop 0.35 → **0.58** (binary-searched floor). Now 4.99 / 7.75 / 12.62.
- **Motion easing corrected** — `--observatory-motion` was Material ease-in-out; at 10% of 160ms it had covered 2.6% of distance. Now `--observatory-ease` quart (34.7%). Also caught that WP-E would have made three hovers snap (transition lists named only `transform` while `:hover` changed `background`).
- **WP-F NOT shipped.** Premise false: `ThemeContext` adds/removes `.light`, so dark is the *absence* of a class, while `classroom.css` keys 22 dark rules on `html.dark` which nothing sets. Fix is whole-classroom, never rendered, needs its own session.
- **Copy pass logged** to `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\wiki\COPY-CORRECTIONS.md` under a 2026-09-09 heading so it cannot be silently reverted.
